{"parentUuid":"0713bb0a-36f7-47a1-9a7e-c341a9a2db0e","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"8a9ea7b2-c6d7-44fa-ac15-131b6674fdae","agentId":"abee79fc04e41b5e9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Vt9FxvvSKr7D3ycQg64Zng","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Unit A2 — adversarial audit of the wiring-sweep follow-up\n2\t\n3\t## Subject and chain\n4\t\n5\tBranch `claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9`, tip `f7d9fd1`, baseline `308f728` (the accepted\n6\thost-split campaign). The chain: W4 (`1a2f04e`) moved the harness wiring to the canon and planned\n7\tthe catalog file; W5 (`6520640`) made superseded canon copies foreign findings, retired the\n8\tadvisory, and pinned the control table; W6 (`fdf72fe`) rewrote the documentation, the visit, the\n9\tquality-rule seam, and closed the pointer template's stale claim. Opus 5 wrote every commit (the\n10\tCodex bench is dark). A lane whose engine is Opus audits its own engine's work: attack that half\n11\tharder.\n12\t\n13\t## What the round decides\n14\t\n15\tWhether the follow-up is accepted and the branch offered to the owner as the release that ends\n16\tinstruction-file propagation with a one-command fleet visit. A finding is worth more than a clean\n17\tpass: after publication every target's `overwrite` runs this deletion.\n18\t\n19\t## Already established — do not re-run\n20\t\n21\tVerified by the Orchestrator directly:\n22\t\n23\t- Every scoped project green at `f7d9fd1`: `src:core` 373, `src:server` 421, `src:bin` 209,\n24\t  `config` 46, `policy` 111, `guides` 17, each exit 0. `npm run build` regenerates `host.json`\n25\t  byte-identically.\n26\t- The end-to-end probe (`.orkestrel/scaffold/probe-sweep.md`): a generated target carries no\n27\t  harness wiring and keeps the catalog file; a tracked canon leftover is a foreign finding and\n28\t  `audit` exits 1; one `overwrite` run repairs the pointers and removes the leftovers (\"3\n29\t  removed\", zero drift); the swept tree's offline audit exits 0. The online stale row against the\n30\t  published release is the overlay working correctly on an unpublished branch.\n31\t- W5's measured bound: a full pre-split canon reports findings and bytes far under every audit\n32\t  limit, and its mutation probe (the `#derive` line removed and restored) reddens exactly the\n33\t  sweep's cases.\n34\t- W4's failing-first record and the byte-stable inventory through the membership move alone.\n35\t\n36\t## Review evidence\n37\t\n38\tThe actual diff: `tmp/units/a2.diff` (campaign folder excluded). The status output:\n39\t`git status --porcelain` empty at dispatch. The reports and plan: `.orkestrel/scaffold/plan2.md`,\n40\t`w4-report.md`, `w5-report.md`, `w6-report.md`, the design lane reports, and the probe records.\n41\tYour lane does not execute; the probe records and unit failing-first records are the executed\n42\tevidence supplied for behavioral claims.\n43\t\n44\t## Numbered falsifiable claims\n45\t\n46\t1. **The membership is total and disjoint.** `CANON_PATHS` holds the harness wiring\n47\t   (`.claude/agents`, `.codex/agents`, `.codex/config.toml`, `.cursor/mcp.json`,\n48\t   `.cursor/rules`, `.mcp.json`) beside the instruction set; `HOST_PATHS` holds only files; no\n49\t   member of either list equals or sits beneath a member of the other; staging carries the union\n50\t   and `host.json` records every member.\n51\t2. **The plan's canon claims are exactly the pointer pair and the catalog file**, each with the\n52\t   origin and ownership the design fixed, and `catalog` still rewrites a generated target's\n53\t   table while `repair` restores the file's absence.\n54\t3. **An unplanned canon path in a target is a group-scoped foreign finding**: `audit` exits 1 on\n55\t   one, a scoped audit outside its groups reports nothing, and no planned path can reach\n56\t   `foreign`.\n57\t4. **One `overwrite` run sweeps clean**: it repairs the pointers and deletes every tracked\n58\t   unplanned canon file through the one existing transaction, sparing planned paths, untracked\n59\t   paths, and `src/`/`app/`; the deletion is by path membership, never byte identity.\n60\t5. **`repair` deletes nothing**, and the dirty-tree refusal and `--dirty` waiver are unchanged.\n61\t6. **The advisory is fully retired**: no `canon` question value, message, or handler remains in\n62\t   `src/`; the fetch-list filter still excludes canon and deferred destinations; the overlay\n63\t   still keeps floor bytes for them.\n64\t7. **`host.json` moved exactly with the staged files the follow-up edited** (the vendored\n65\t   `guides/scaffold.md`, and the canon `quality.md`, `orkestrel.md`, and wave reference) plus\n66\t   the membership digest — no entry added, removed, or reordered across the follow-up.\n67\t8. **No file scaffold plans into a target names a path the target lacks**: the pointer bodies,\n68\t   the catalog agent file, and `.claude/settings.json` with its hook scripts name only paths a\n69\t   generated target holds or the scaffold-side locations the pointer semantics fix.\n70\t9. **The shipped prose is true**: the guide, README, roadmap, wave reference, and\n71\t   `.claude/rules/quality.md` state the shipped verb split; no sentence claims a verb never\n72\t   removes a superseded copy; the `prove` seam is stated as registration outside the repository\n73\t   with the in-tree registration's standing-finding consequence documented; the visit is re-pin,\n74\t   `scaffold overwrite`, gates.\n75\t10. **The law holds across the diff**: no suppression, `any`, or assertion; naming, placement,\n76\t    and centralization respected; no count in added prose over a growable set; every public\n77\t    export documented and barrel-reachable; the writing law's banned terms absent from added\n78\t    sentences.\n79\t11. **The package is coherent as a whole** — one design from membership to visit. Would you ship\n80\t    this to the fleet?\n81\t\n82\t## Instructions\n83\t\n84\t- Attempt refutation. `CONFIRMED` requires naming the attack you tried that failed; a claim you\n85\t  cannot decide is `UNRESOLVED` with what would settle it. Do not hedge toward an imagined\n86\t  consensus; you run blind.\n87\t- Assume the chain has one more defect. The freshest surface is the writers' own rulings: the\n88\t  `#derive` canon source's group gate, the fleet-fixture change (`buildFleetManifest` emitting\n89\t  the catalog file), the retired advisory's removed imports, the rewritten invariant sentences,\n90\t  and the re-baselined registration seam.\n91\t- Your lane edits nothing and spawns nothing. Findings outside the claims enter the verdict only\n92\t  substantiated to the `BROKEN` standard.\n93\t\n94\t## Output\n95\t\n96\tThe `orkestrel-falsify` verdict shape: numbered per-claim verdicts with evidence, substantiated\n97\tfindings outside the claims, and the single terminal line. No process diary.\n98\t"}]},"uuid":"9202462c-75d3-46d4-bf1b-d3f424659f99","timestamp":"2026-08-27T09:28:20.596Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"0713bb0a-36f7-47a1-9a7e-c341a9a2db0e","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}
{"parentUuid":"e2b27452-c9bb-45ac-897f-67547c72030d","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"8a9ea7b2-c6d7-44fa-ac15-131b6674fdae","agentId":"abee79fc04e41b5e9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01RaEtBMRXFq7ZLDpwcf1Uur","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t---\n2\tname: orkestrel-falsify\n3\tdescription: Run an adversarial audit round against work that already looks finished — write the subject as numbered falsifiable claims, dispatch independent auditors instructed to break them rather than confirm them, reconcile their evidence, and rule. Use before accepting a fix round, before a version bump or publication, when green gates are the only evidence a change works, when a defect has recurred across rounds, or whenever a review would otherwise read a diff and agree with it.\n4\t---\n5\t\n6\t# Falsify\n7\t\n8\tGreen gates prove a suite ran. They prove nothing about the claim the work makes. This skill is for\n9\tthe moment when everything passes and the work is still not known to be right.\n10\t\n11\t## Load authority\n12\t\n13\t1. `AGENTS.md`.\n14\t2. `.claude/rules/quality.md`, especially **Falsification** — the laws governing auditor behaviour.\n15\t   This skill prescribes the round; that section prescribes the conduct inside it. Do not restate it.\n16\t3. The governing guide/spec for the subject.\n17\t4. `references/brief.md` before writing a brief; `references/reconcile.md` before ruling.\n18\t\n19\t## When a round is warranted\n20\t\n21\tRun one when the work is finished and the evidence is weak in a specific way:\n22\t\n23\t- gates are green and nothing has attacked the claim;\n24\t- a fix round is about to be accepted, especially one whose author reported success;\n25\t- a version is about to be bumped, packed, or published;\n26\t- the same defect class has now appeared in more than one round;\n27\t- a self-declared \"sound, unchanged, no fix needed\" verdict is load-bearing.\n28\t\n29\tDo not run one for a typo, a mechanical rename, or work whose failure would be immediately visible.\n30\t\n31\tA round also needs something new to attack. When the previous round's claims all held, nothing has\n32\tbeen added or repaired since, and the only motive is that an auditor could still imagine an attack,\n33\tthere is no subject — closing is the correct action and the next subject is the deliverable.\n34\t\n35\tWhen the same class has recurred along one seam to the budget in `.claude/rules/quality.md`\n36\t§ Rounds and verdicts, the next move is that law's strategy switch — the breadth sweep of the\n37\tstream, the ruling, or the dropped moving-target claim — rather than another successor round.\n38\t\n39\t## Write the brief\n40\t\n41\tThe brief is the instrument. A weak brief produces a confirming review no matter which auditor reads\n42\tit. Follow `references/brief.md`. What this skill adds beyond the conduct law:\n43\t\n44\t- Every re-run is a **successor**, not a restatement. It carries what the previous round closed so\n45\t  nothing is re-reported, and it adds claims that attack **the previous round's own rulings**.\n46\t- The brief names what the round **decides**. An auditor that does not know the stakes calibrates to\n47\t  politeness.\n48\t- Unknowns are named as unknowns, with how the auditor reports back on them.\n49\t\n50\tThe claim form itself is the Falsification law's — read it there. The **verdict shape** below is this\n51\tskill's, because `.agents/orchestration.md` assigns it here; everything else about auditor conduct is the law's.\n52\t\n53\t## Evidence, by subject type\n54\t\n55\tThe brief supplies the evidence its subject actually has. Requiring a diff for a subject that has no\n56\tdiff is a rule the brief cannot satisfy.\n57\t\n58\t| subject                                 | required evidence                                                                     |\n59\t| --------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n60\t| a code change                           | the actual diff and the actual status output; omitting either is a dispatch deviation |\n61\t| a rendered or externally driven surface | the capture portfolio as primary, source as corroboration                             |\n62\t| a policy, design, or process proposal   | the proposal, the canon it must satisfy, and the record of what motivated it          |\n63\t\n64\t**A subject can occupy more than one row; supply every row it occupies.** A ruling whose fixes\n65\talready landed as edits is both a proposal and a code change, and withholding the diff on the grounds\n66\tthat the subject is \"a proposal\" leaves the auditor unable to check whether a fix changed anything\n67\tnobody claimed.\n68\t\n69\t## Run the round\n70\t\n71\t- **Verify every authority the brief references exists in the tree the auditor is rooted in.** A\n72\t  brief that points at a rule file, section, or guide the executor cannot find delivers nothing while\n73\t  looking like authority — and it fails silently, because an auditor does not report a heading it\n74\t  never saw. Check before dispatch; propagate the missing file rather than restating its contents in\n75\t  the brief. This is the reason restatement felt necessary, and it is the wrong cure.\n76\t- Run the **adversarial pass** on one identical brief: a subjective lane and an objective\n77\t  lane, each a fresh subagent with a clean context, blind to each other. Reconcile them yourself.\n78\t  `.agents/orchestration.md` owns lane definitions, engine assignment, and what happens when an\n79\t  engine is dark; do not restate them here.\n80\t- A round run with one lane is a deviation. Record it rather than glossing it. If an engine is\n81\t  unavailable, the remaining engine runs every lane — it never drops one.\n82\t- **Pair every finder with an independent refuter when the round fans out past the subjective and\n83\t  objective lanes.** The\n84\t  refuter receives one slice's findings, never that finder's work, and is briefed to BREAK them\n85\t  rather than to re-audit the subject. It reproduces each stated vector itself and defaults to\n86\t  refuted when uncertain.\n87\t- Refute on any of these grounds, and name which: the vector does not reproduce; the behaviour is\n88\t  correct and documented; it is unreachable through the public API or a documented seam; it asks\n89\t  for new capability rather than naming a defect; it restates a finding an earlier round\n90\t  repaired; or its diagnosis is wrong — then CONFIRM with the correction.\n91\t- Only a survivor earns a fix unit. An unrefuted finding is a hypothesis. The scope grounds,\n92\t  unreachable and new capability, are what keep a round from drifting into a redesign.\n93\t- **Give every auditor the means to run its attacks.** A lens that can only read returns derivations,\n94\t  and a derivation reads exactly like a verdict — it will confirm a claim that one probe would break.\n95\t- **Tell each auditor exactly where a probe may live, and verify that place works before you say it.**\n96\t  A test runner resolves only what its own configuration includes: a probe written outside every\n97\t  configured project is not discovered at all, and the run reports no test files rather than a result.\n98\t  Where the repository provides a probe project, use it and name that project in the brief;\n99\t  `.claude/rules/tests.md` governs what may live there. Where it does not, the reliable form is a file\n100\t  inside the canonical mirrored suite, run by explicit path. Either way the probe is deleted before the\n101\t  auditor returns, and promoted into a permanent test when it proves something worth keeping. Give each\n102\t  concurrent auditor a distinct filename that already satisfies the repository's test naming\n103\t  convention; never invent a prefix to dodge collisions, and never let two auditors claim one path. A\n104\t  probe left in the mirrored suite is discovered and fails a run nobody else caused.\n105\t- **Run auditors concurrently only when their writes cannot collide.** A lens that can execute\n106\t  still writes probes; give each a distinct path and forbid whole-project runs, or serialize the\n107\t  round. **This binds\n108\t  the orchestrator too:** a tree-wide gate run while a round is live sees the auditors' in-flight probes\n109\t  and reports a failure nobody caused. Wait for the round, or scope the command to paths no auditor\n110\t  owns. Never delete another executor's working file to make your own command pass.\n111\t- **Tell a lane when its own engine wrote the half it is auditing, and tell it to attack that half\n112\t  harder.** A fix round reviewed by the engine that wrote it is the case the round exists to avoid,\n113\t  and where the pass cannot avoid it, naming it is what recovers the round. A clean pass on its own\n114\t  engine's work is the least valuable result a lane can return.\n115\t- Supply the evidence the subject type requires, per the table above.\n116\t- Auditors edit no source and spawn nothing. Read-only describes the SUBJECT, never the lane's\n117\t  tools.\n118\t- **Read the lane's allowlist before writing its brief.** A lane with no write tool cannot create\n119\t  a probe; a lane with no exec tool cannot run one or read `git`. Naming either stops the unit on\n120\t  arrival over a detail the allowlist already settled.\n121\t- Where the lane cannot execute, run the probe yourself, record its control and its output, and\n122\t  supply that record as the lane's evidence. The Orchestrator produces, the lane rules. Never\n123\t  widen a lane's tools to fit a brief.\n124\t- Blind reports are **immutable**. Nothing an auditor returns is edited, merged, or revised — by\n125\t  anyone, including the auditor — once it has been returned.\n126\t\n127\t## Verdict shape\n128\t\n129\tEvery auditor in every round returns exactly this, and nothing else. One shape makes rounds\n130\tcomparable; a round that invents its own cannot be read against the last one.\n131\t\n132\t1. **Numbered verdicts**, one per claim, in the brief's order. Exactly one of these values:\n133\t\n134\t   | value           | meaning                                                                                | evidence                                                                                                                                                      |\n135\t   | --------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n136\t   | `CONFIRMED`     | attacked and it held                                                                   | as the Falsification law requires                                                                                                                             |\n137\t   | `BROKEN`        | falsified                                                                              | as the Falsification law requires — note it says _input, **state, or interleaving**_, so a concurrency claim is falsified by an interleaving, not by an input |\n138\t   | `UNRESOLVED`    | cannot be decided from the evidence available                                          | what would settle it                                                                                                                                          |\n139\t   | `NOT-EVIDENCED` | a claim about a rendered or externally driven surface the supplied capture cannot show | which capture is missing                                                                                                                                      |\n140\t\n141\t   `CONFIRMED` and `BROKEN` defer; only `UNRESOLVED` and `NOT-EVIDENCED` are this skill's, because\n142\t   the law does not name them. `BROKEN` and `UNRESOLVED` are **separate**: a claim nobody could\n143\t   decide has not been falsified, and it cannot supply the fields falsification requires.\n144\t   `NOT-EVIDENCED` is the token the `analyst` and `reviewer` charters already require; it is kept,\n145\t   not re-invented.\n146\t\n147\t2. **Findings fitting no claim**, if any, each substantiated to the same standard as `BROKEN`.\n148\t\n149\t3. **One terminal line**, and only one:\n150\t\n151\t   ```text\n152\t   VERDICT: PASS — <m> of <m> confirmed, no findings outside the claims\n153\t   VERDICT: FAIL — <n> broken, <u> unresolved, <e> not-evidenced, <x> findings outside the claims\n154\t   ```\n155\t\n156\t   **`PASS` requires every one of these to be true**: every claim `CONFIRMED`, nothing `UNRESOLVED`, nothing\n157\t   `NOT-EVIDENCED`, and no substantiated finding outside the claims. A single substantiated finding\n158\t   forces `FAIL` no matter how the numbered claims landed — otherwise a round can report a real\n159\t   defect and still emit the word that authorises the release.\n160\t\n161\tNo process diary. No summary of what was read.\n162\t\n163\t## When a round comes back all-confirmed\n164\t\n165\tThe Falsification law governs this: the brief goes on trial, not the subject. Apply it, and if the\n166\tclaims turn out to have been descriptive, the successor brief is where the sharper ones go.\n167\t\n168\t## Reconcile and rule\n169\t\n170\tFollow `references/reconcile.md`. The obligations that are not delegable:\n171\t\n172\t- **Reproduce every sharp finding yourself** before acting on it. An auditor's finding is a\n173\t  hypothesis until the orchestrator has run it.\n174\t- **Build before you pack.** `npm pack` runs no build, so it ships whatever `dist/` is on disk. An\n175\t  auditor who packs an artifact to inspect it is reading the last build, not the current source, and\n176\t  will report deleted exports as still shipping. Run the package's build first, and say in the brief\n177\t  that the tarball was built from the commit under audit.\n178\t- **Check the subject before acting on a finding, not only the reasoning.** A brief that names attack\n179\t  vectors teaches the lane those vectors matter, and a lane can hand back the brief's own questions as\n180\t  the subject's claims — demanding coverage for a property the subject never documented. Grep the\n181\t  subject for the claim the finding rests on. Where it is not there, the finding is against the brief.\n182\t- **A disagreement between auditors is rarely a tie to average.** It is usually correct answers\n183\t  to different questions. Find the question each one answered.\n184\t- **Bound every finding**: state what is _not_ broken, and why the adjacent behaviour that looks the\n185\t  same is correct. An audit that reports everything is as useless as one that reports nothing.\n186\t- **Bound the fix before briefing it.** Establish what over-correcting would break, and include that\n187\t  in the fix brief as a constraint.\n188\t- Drop, on the record, any finding neither auditor can substantiate.\n189\t\n190\t## Accept, or run it again\n191\t\n192\tThe threshold is **a `PASS` terminal line on a brief whose claims cover what the subject owns** —\n193\tevery numbered claim `CONFIRMED` on evidence, nothing `UNRESOLVED`, nothing `NOT-EVIDENCED`, no\n194\tsubstantiated finding beside them. Never green gates, which prove only that a suite ran.\n195\t\n196\tA round that finds something is a success, not a delay. The alternative is a consumer finding it\n197\tafter publication, when the version number is already spent. But an unsubstantiated attack is not a\n198\tfinding, and the supply of imaginable ones never runs out: a round is not re-run because an auditor\n199\tcan still think of one. A substantiated finding against something no claim names is real and forces\n200\t`FAIL`; an unsubstantiated one is a claim for the successor brief. The escalation law in\n201\t`.claude/rules/quality.md` governs a subject that keeps producing findings at the same seam — after\n202\tenough of them the ruling owed is on the design, not on the next defect.\n203\t\n204\tWhen a fix round follows, its auditor must be an engine that did not write it, and the next round's\n205\tbrief is the successor of this one.\n206\t"}]},"uuid":"140fa05a-3be1-4f3c-be0f-1a545e58b3cf","timestamp":"2026-08-27T09:28:20.602Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"e2b27452-c9bb-45ac-897f-67547c72030d","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}
{"parentUuid":"140fa05a-3be1-4f3c-be0f-1a545e58b3cf","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"abee79fc04e41b5e9","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CeT1upP1L2HBGEPJey2He","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"","signature":"CAIS5AIKpgEIERgCKkCTLctBnQi5+9qkykSzq7y2Z33odOW65eqloZdDRwOKyn331yuUobjoRTUKSH9cC1L0YtmXgyKkigNkFMHuAcwJMg1jbGF1ZGUtb3B1cy01OAFCCHRoaW5raW5nWiQzZTE1YWQ3Yy1mNDllLTQ3NGUtOWE0OC0wNjkzYzQ1MjQzOTlyEOSFBjb8ynXhp66iSZvXzt2IAQGoAbaGwNQGsAECEgxf/tVvnoxwAfrYxxkaDLiDeKTq1qAmrsCZ8yIwGkNmYDKbn78Smr5xd2wlKZSmmFSxSpTof3A8o+DLLeSPp1cJS9y0oB46iMbd5eboKmt54C7bXsdjGP1FqUzkoNZTKiy67G38iBbAQ93Y9sBJGpak28NRS/TTJOM5Tk/ykBwjWL+Xrg2/HXu6I+FVSmPRBDI9CrLGaF+ha98K/D494ovvLtuS/oYsPoX8T7ibq7oRn7WW5W3OM0XuUxgB"}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":16481,"cache_read_input_tokens":36280,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":16481,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":3,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011CeT1uoaPS9vfwyKgSzYie","attributionAgent":"reviewer","type":"assistant","uuid":"78d641d7-6062-46ae-b111-a104aa20a3fd","timestamp":"2026-08-27T09:28:22.777Z","effort":"high","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}
{"parentUuid":"78d641d7-6062-46ae-b111-a104aa20a3fd","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"abee79fc04e41b5e9","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CeT1upP1L2HBGEPJey2He","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01GfjjB79vKw4f2F4SmJ2xwT","name":"Read","input":{"file_path":"/home/user/scaffold/.claude/rules/quality.md"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":16481,"cache_read_input_tokens":36280,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":16481,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":3,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011CeT1uoaPS9vfwyKgSzYie","attributionAgent":"reviewer","type":"assistant","uuid":"b0cc6416-b9e8-477b-adb7-c86af1f2ba65","timestamp":"2026-08-27T09:28:23.234Z","effort":"high","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}
{"parentUuid":"b0cc6416-b9e8-477b-adb7-c86af1f2ba65","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"8a9ea7b2-c6d7-44fa-ac15-131b6674fdae","agentId":"abee79fc04e41b5e9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01GfjjB79vKw4f2F4SmJ2xwT","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t---\n2\tpaths:\n3\t  - 'src/**/*'\n4\t  - 'app/**/*'\n5\t  - 'tests/**/*'\n6\t  - 'guides/**/*'\n7\t  - 'package.json'\n8\t  - 'vite.config.ts'\n9\t  - 'tsconfig.json'\n10\t  - '.agents/skills/**/*'\n11\t  - '.claude/skills/**/*'\n12\t---\n13\t\n14\t# Research, hardening, and completion rules\n15\t\n16\t## Evidence before change\n17\t\n18\t- Research when the user asks for it, when comparing an upstream, protocol, or legacy implementation, or when current external behavior materially affects design.\n19\t- Use current primary sources for external capabilities, and the exact installed declarations and guides for dependencies. Separate verified fact from inference.\n20\t- Read authoritative types and named decision-bearing implementation files first-hand. Delegate bulk supporting context, never the owning design decision.\n21\t- Treat existing code, tests, `old/`, branches, and copied projects as evidence, not authority.\n22\t- Build a capability/defect matrix before a broad API or production-readiness change. Every row ends as implement, repair, retain, or intentionally exclude with evidence.\n23\t- Fix that matrix when the change starts. It is the definition of done, and the change closes when every row closes.\n24\t- Never end a row as \"hardened further.\" Replace any evaluative phrase with the concrete condition that closes the row.\n25\t- Record a finding outside the matrix against the row that owns it, for the next matrix. Do not reopen this one.\n26\t\n27\t## Probes before arguments\n28\t\n29\tA question a probe can settle is settled by the probe, whether or not anyone has disputed it. Reasoning decides what to run and what the result means. It does not replace the run.\n30\t\n31\t- Test your own assumptions before you rely on them. You do not need a disagreement to justify a probe. If you are about to depend on what a function returns, what a config resolves to, what a flag does, or whether a path is even reached, run it and find out.\n32\t- Treat a long deliberation about behaviour as the signal to stop and run something. Deliberation that a ten-line probe would have ended is the most expensive habit in this process, and it is invisible because it feels like rigour.\n33\t- Verify a belief before stating it, or label it as unverified. An unverified assertion put into context becomes a fact for everything downstream, including other agents, and correcting it later costs more than the check would have.\n34\t- Hold a claim you issue to the standard you hold a claim you receive. Nothing downstream re-checks an instruction, so an unverified fact inside one becomes the premise of every task built on it. Auditing every report you receive while exempting every brief you write is the common form of this failure.\n35\t- Prefer the check over the chain of reasoning whenever the check is cheap. Reading the installed declaration, running one line, or counting the call sites beats three paragraphs inferring the same thing, and it produces evidence you can hand to someone else.\n36\t- Write the probe before the argument gets long. The moment a claim about behaviour is disputed — between engines, between a report and a rule, or in your own head — stop and run it. Get the smallest real input through the real code and read the real output.\n37\t- Bound the search before starting it, and put the bound in the brief. Name the benchmark, the population, or the row that ends it. An investigation with no stated stopping condition runs until attention runs out. This includes the case where the honest answer is that the limit is inherent and belongs in documentation rather than in code.\n38\t- Treat a negative probe as evidence about the probe until its input is shown to reach the code under test. A pass proves nothing if the vector never arrived. Instrument the path, assert an observable side effect, or drive it through a door you can see.\n39\t- When a report names a defect and your reproduction comes back clean, assume first that your vector was weaker than theirs, and go get their exact vector. Treating a failed reproduction as a disproof is the most common way a true finding is lost.\n40\t- A reported vector the compiler rejects refutes the VECTOR, never the finding. Where a claim asserts reachability through a typed API, compile the exact vector under the project's own settings; re-derive one the types admit before dropping the finding, and record which vector was actually tested.\n41\t- Reproduce a reported defect's cause before instructing a fix from it. A real symptom can carry a wrong diagnosis, and a fix aimed at the stated cause edits the wrong file while the defect survives.\n42\t- Do not read a result into a tool's failure to find your probe. \"No tests found\", an empty match, a skipped file, a runner that resolved nothing — these report on the harness, not the subject. Confirm the probe was collected and executed first.\n43\t- Prefer an observation over a derivation, including your own. When a measurement and an argument disagree, the argument is wrong until the measurement is shown to be broken.\n44\t- Diagnose from the artifact the work produces — the file, the count, the exit code, the timestamp on what changed — never from a wrapper or a proxy signal that merely correlates with progress. Read a gate bare: a pipeline stage after it (`| tail`, `| grep`) reports the stage's exit status and hides the gate's failing lines.\n45\t- Verify a comment or an agent's report against the call sites before relying on it. A code comment is not evidence. When lanes disagree about whether a path is live, count the callers rather than weighing the prose.\n46\t- What a round proves is what it ran. A conclusion carried from one door to another is a hypothesis at the second door. Re-run it there.\n47\t\n48\t## Falsification\n49\t\n50\tA review that reads a diff finds what the diff shows. A review that tries to break named claims finds what the diff hides. Code that has passed diff review several times can still carry a defect nobody has tried to trigger.\n51\t\n52\t### Writing the claims\n53\t\n54\t- State the subject as a numbered list of the claims the work makes, never as \"review this diff.\" Each claim is falsifiable: a property some concrete input, state, or interleaving could show false.\n55\t- Instruct the auditor to attempt refutation, not confirmation. A claim it cannot break is CONFIRMED with the evidence that convinced it. A claim it breaks is BROKEN with the exact failing input, state, or interleaving, plus the smallest correct fix.\n56\t- Derive claims from what the change asserts under adverse conditions: cancellation, restart, concurrency, partial failure, hostile input, resource exhaustion, and the orderings a happy path never reaches.\n57\t- Read the installed declaration or implementation of every substrate a claim depends on. A claim about `stop()` is unfalsifiable until you know what `stop()` does when the status is not the one the caller assumed.\n58\t\n59\t### Instruments\n60\t\n61\t- An instrument is not evidence until it has failed. Pair every probe, comparison, or matrix with a negative control that must report failure, run under the same conditions. An identity check whose control reports \"same\" has measured nothing.\n62\t- When a question about a TypeScript edit can supply a workspace project, a case of workspace files with a test, and a negative control naming its files, its test, the stage it must fail at, and why, call the `prove` tool the `probe` MCP server registers before relying on the answer. When the question supplies no project, no case, or no control, follow `.claude/rules/tests.md` § Probes and report the fallback instrument's own control and coverage.\n63\t- When no `probe` server is registered in the session, register one outside the repository, in the harness's own local or user MCP scope — in Claude Code, `claude mcp add` outside project scope — naming the installed `node_modules/@orkestrel/probe/dist/bin/main.js` entry, and start it in the repository whose projects the question names, because the server fixes its workspace from its own working directory. The registration cannot live in the tree: a scaffold target holds no `.mcp.json` file, that path is instruction canon, and a copy at it reports as foreign drift on every `scaffold audit` run. Where the harness registers no server at all, treat the question as supplying no project and take the preceding rule's fallback.\n64\t- Quote the closing line of the `prove` answer verbatim in every report, brief, and audit verdict that rests on the claim: the `receipt probe:<digest>:…` line when the case ran clean and the control broke exactly where the claim declared it would, and the `no receipt` line otherwise. A `no receipt` line leaves the claim unproved — report it with the stage that refused.\n65\t- Read a receipt as evidence about its claim, never as a gate result. The gate chain still runs, and `verifier` still owns its result.\n66\t- Draw the negative control from outside the population the instrument covers. Name the instrument's membership rule first, then pick a control that rule excludes. A control sampled from constructs the instrument already handles proves only that it discriminates among those constructs, and says nothing about the class it silently cannot reach.\n67\t- State an instrument's coverage beside its result. A conclusion inherits the instrument's scope, not the question's. An unstated coverage claim is read as complete, and it never is. A search proves something about the paths it walked, so name them.\n68\t- Match the instrument to the question. A text search reports on text, so a claim about declarations, call sites, or structure needs the compiler or a parser instead. A pattern written for one spelling of a construct reports on that spelling alone. A path check answers relative to the directory it runs from, so resolve the inputs against their own base before reading a miss as a finding.\n69\t- Name the rival reading the instrument must exclude, and show it reports differently under that reading. Give independent measurements independent state: one counter shared across members reports read order and per-member read count identically, so a result consistent with both measured neither.\n70\t- Report a question unanswered rather than answering it with a weaker instrument. A fallback that measures something adjacent returns a confident wrong answer, and nothing downstream can tell that answer from the real one — searching commit messages for a release when the question is where a version changed will match some release, just not the one asked about. Name the substitute and what it actually measures, or say the question is open.\n71\t- State what the controls established and what they did not. An instrument certified only from the inside is trusted exactly where it has never been tested.\n72\t- Treat a gap between what an instrument says it checks and what it actually matches as a defect in the instrument, not as a documented limit. A recorded blind spot buys trust only when everything outside it is genuinely covered.\n73\t- Measure the product, not the harness. A recorded baseline that counts something about its own fixture is not evidence about the shipped surface, however often a guide quotes it.\n74\t- Baseline a published-artifact claim against the published artifact. \"Did my change move the surface\" and \"does this release differ from the last one\" are different questions, and a diff against your own starting point answers only the first. A toolchain that re-emits declarations moves the artifact without any source edit, so every writer can correctly report an unmoved surface while the package's published contract has changed. Fetch what consumers actually have — the tarball, the deployed asset — and compare against that.\n75\t- Prove a module cycle by loading the built artifact, not by a green suite. Tests import through the source graph and a bundler resolves it differently, so a cycle that is fatal at module-init in the shipped form can stay invisible under every test. Import each published entry point and read an export from it.\n76\t- Adopt an instrument that settled a claim as a test before accepting the work it settled. The probe that proved a fix, carrying the control that proved the probe, is that fix's regression guard. A verification that runs once is a rehearsal, not a gate.\n77\t\n78\t### Rounds and verdicts\n79\t\n80\t- Treat an all-confirmed round as a legitimate result, and put the brief on trial rather than the subject. Re-read the claims and ask whether any could have been falsified by evidence the round actually had. If none could, the claims were descriptive and the round proved nothing — sharpen them and re-run. If they could have been and were not, the pass stands.\n81\t- Name the claims you could not break either way, so the next round knows what has already been attacked.\n82\t- Never tell an auditor that a clean round means it did not try. That instructs it to manufacture a finding, and a manufactured finding costs a fix unit, an argument, and the credibility of the true findings beside it.\n83\t- Treat a repaired claim as a new claim, not a settled one. Re-ask it at every entry point that reaches the same rule, not only the door the defect arrived through. The engine that wrote the fix is least able to see this, because re-verifying where the fix is feels like verifying the fix.\n84\t- A fix that adopts the auditor's prescription verbatim may close with a mutation probe in place of a fresh audit round: disable the load-bearing line, watch the adopted pin fail, restore it, and commit the pin as the regression guard. A fix that departs from the prescription gets the cross-engine round.\n85\t- Let reachability bound the fix. A defect reachable through the package's own shipped code or a documented extension seam falsifies its claim and is repaired now.\n86\t- Document the obligation instead when a defect is reachable only through a hypothetical foreign implementation of a contract this package publishes. State it on the interface that owns it and prove the documentation. Do not build coordination machinery against a requirement nobody wrote down. Attacks are unlimited; reachable ones are not, and only the reachable set is a work list.\n87\t- **Three rounds at one seam is the budget, and reaching it switches the search strategy rather than stopping the work.** Repeated rounds against one seam are evidence about the design, not evidence of diligence. At the third round, name what the audit is trying to accomplish, then pick the successor strategy from what the recurrence shows.\n88\t- Recurrence with a direction — each fix relocates the class along one stream, or the evidence points up a dependency chain — ends the depth search. Run one breadth round that probes the stream's stations in parallel to locate the source, in the shape `.agents/orchestration.md` § Context and decomposition prescribes, then plan the downstream work from the source and size it by the sweep's map.\n89\t- Recurrence with no direction makes the seam itself the question. The next unit is a ruling — on the threat model, the mechanism, or the boundary — taken with the same adversarial pass a design gets, not a fourth repair.\n90\t- A subject that reprices itself on every edit — a count, a census, a total over prose — has no closing condition and is not a seam. Drop the claim, or recast it as the property the tally stood in for.\n91\t- Write the round count down in the capability/defect matrix row that owns the seam, when the seam opens, so it is a fact rather than a feeling. A seam that has consumed more rounds than the rest of the matrix combined has already answered the question.\n92\t- State the ruling that ends a seam as the invariant the code will obey, the constraint bounding it against over-correction, and the interface where a consumer meets the obligation. A ruling that names only the defect it replaces produces the opposite defect next round.\n93\t- Give every behavioural audit the means to run its attacks. An auditor that cannot execute cannot falsify a behavioural claim: it returns derivations, and a derivation reads exactly like a verdict while being a different thing — it will confirm a claim one probe would break. Treat a report with no executed evidence as a review of the source, and label it as such.\n94\t\n95\t## Ecosystem reuse\n96\t\n97\tThe root laws on inspecting declared `@orkestrel/*` capabilities, reusing a matching primitive, and updating every consumer without shims bind here without restatement. This file adds the judgment calls:\n98\t\n99\t- Prove the semantic difference before keeping a local variant. Similar names are not evidence of different behavior.\n100\t- Treat downstream friction as valid evidence of a reusable upstream defect, not as automatic proof. Fix the lowest package that owns the general mechanism and keep product policy downstream.\n101\t- Never re-export a dependency's symbol to soften a consumer's import.\n102\t\n103\t## Production hardening\n104\t\n105\t- Translate \"enterprise-grade\" or \"production-ready\" into an explicit risk and seam matrix covering applicable inputs, states, failures, cleanup, cancellation, concurrency, resource ownership, hostile boundaries, environment isolation, serialization and restore, and package consumption.\n106\t- Grade that matrix on coverage, not optimization: whether every applicable seam exists, works, and stays proven. Do not grade on how many further interleavings can be invented against the one seam already proven. A surface polished past its row buys less than the next uncovered seam would have.\n107\t- Test observable invariants at each applicable seam with real implementations.\n108\t- Use dedicated real-service projects for external model or service behavior. Require readiness and tune each request to the smallest robust proof.\n109\t- Audit test discovery, counts, skipped and todo tests, cleanup, and assertion adequacy. Passing discovered tests alone is insufficient.\n110\t- Inspect public exports, declarations, supported runtime targets, and generated outputs.\n111\t- Treat a claim that a surface works with an external client as unproven until one representative real client of that class has driven it end to end. Protocol tests prove the protocol, not the integration.\n112\t- Add an independent adversarial review for security, destructive paths, concurrency, protocols, or untrusted external input.\n113\t\n114\t## Completion\n115\t\n116\tThe root completion law — finish every in-scope capability now, leave no TODO, deferral, or hidden follow-up, and run the applicable repository skill for comprehensive work — binds here without restatement. This file adds these obligations:\n117\t\n118\t- Perform a final centralization, wrapper, test-helper, and text-integrity sweep after implementation and before gates.\n119\t- Produce local quality gates and relevant output inspection as required evidence.\n120\t- Stop once the enumerated scope is closed and those gates are green. Stopping there is correct, and the next scope is the deliverable. A further pass over the same surface requires a new instruction from the user, not an auditor's remaining appetite. Depth is owed to what the scope names, not to what an engine can still imagine about it.\n121\t"}]},"uuid":"96ef080a-c601-4d31-be66-ec948ffc5077","timestamp":"2026-08-27T09:28:23.243Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"b0cc6416-b9e8-477b-adb7-c86af1f2ba65","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}

Planned paths pair with their artifacts and never\n19\t   reach `foreign`. `Materializer.remove` then deletes the tracked ones unchanged — one candidate\n20\t   list, one transaction, one `removed` array — and `#canonQuestion` goes, because a second\n21\t   channel naming the same files is a duplicate that drifts. The objective lane's rejection of\n22\t   this route rested on the advisory staying, which the owner's less-machinery instruction\n23\t   overrides; its concerns survive as controls: a non-git target still sees leftovers as audit\n24\t   findings, untracked files are never deleted, and a scoped audit stays silent outside its\n25\t   groups.\n26\t3. **The behavioural consequence is deliberate and documented**: a target holding a superseded\n27\t   copy moves from exit 0 with an advisory to exit 1 with a finding. That is the fleet visit's\n28\t   mechanical success condition.\n29\t4. **The maintainer's seam**: an ignored `.mcp.json` survives every visit (untracked paths are\n30\t   outside `remove`'s law and ignored paths are outside the dirty refusal), which is how a target\n31\t   restores the `prove` server locally. The limit lands once, in `.claude/rules/quality.md`\n32\t   § Instruments; the guide's Limits entry points at the same fact.\n33\t5. **Deletion is by path membership, never byte identity** — identity and everything else is configuration.\n/home/user/scaffold/src/core/helpers.ts-235- * A vendored path and a foreign path found in a target are classified here\n/home/user/scaffold/src/core/helpers.ts-236- * alike, so the plan and the audit never disagree about what a path is.\n/home/user/scaffold/src/core/helpers.ts-237- *\n/home/user/scaffold/src/core/helpers.ts-238- * @example\n/home/user/scaffold/src/core/helpers.ts-239- * ```ts\n/home/user/scaffold/src/core/helpers.ts-240- * import { inferGroup } from '@orkestrel/scaffold'\n/home/user/scaffold/src/core/helpers.ts-241- *\n/home/user/scaffold/src/core/helpers.ts-242- * inferGroup('src/core/index.ts') // 'source'\n/home/user/scaffold/src/core/helpers.ts-243- * inferGroup('AGENTS.md') // 'docs'\n/home/user/scaffold/src/core/helpers.ts-244- * inferGroup('.editorconfig') // 'configs'\n/home/user/scaffold/src/core/helpers.ts-245- * ```\n/home/user/scaffold/src/core/helpers.ts-246- */\nsrc/core/helpers.ts:247:export function inferGroup(path: string): Group {\n/home/user/scaffold/src/core/helpers.ts-248-\tif (path === 'package.json' || path === 'package-lock.json') return 'manifest'\n/home/user/scaffold/src/core/helpers.ts-249-\tif (matchesOrchestrationPath(path)) return 'orchestration'\n/home/user/scaffold/src/core/helpers.ts-250-\tif (path.startsWith('src/') || path.startsWith('app/')) return 'source'\n/home/user/scaffold/src/core/helpers.ts-251-\tif (path.startsWith('tests/')) return 'tests'\n/home/user/scaffold/src/core/helpers.ts-252-\tif (path.startsWith('guides/')) return 'guides'\n/home/user/scaffold/src/core/helpers.ts-253-\tif (path.startsWith('docs/')) return 'docs'\n/home/user/scaffold/src/core/helpers.ts-254-\t// The row holds the licence, the workspace's own front page, and the\n/home/user/scaffold/src/core/helpers.ts-255-\t// scaffold-owned root pointers. The later Node\n1988\t\t// \\`it.runIf\\` predicates retire each matching Node drive for a face published\n1989\t\t// later, which leaves nothing measuring it. So it reddens here and names the\n1990\t\t// subpath a browser branch is owed for. A directory entry vendors\n118\t * everything beneath it.\n119\t *\n120\t * A plan carries the subset its target selects, which is why the list is a\n121\t * candidate set rather than a plan: a workspace never mirrors its own guide.\n122\t *\n123\t * Neither the instruction canon nor the harness wiring is here. A target reads\n124\t * its rules, its skills, its agent roles, its bench configuration, and its MCP\n125\t * registrations from {@link CANON_PATHS} inside the installed package, so no\n126\t * file scaffold leaves in a target names a path the target does not hold.\n127\t * `nameToHostArtifacts` appends {@link CATALOG_AGENT_PATH} to what this list\n128\t * selects, which is what keeps the list itself disjoint from the canon.\n129\t */\n130\texport const HOST_PATHS: readonly string[] = Object.freeze([\n131\t\t'LICENSE',\n132\t\t'.claude/settings.json',\n133\t\t'scripts/deps.sh',\n134\t\t'scripts/cursor.sh',\n135\t\t'scripts/codex.sh',\n136\t\t'scripts/ollama.sh',\n137\t\t'tests/setupPolicy.ts',\n138\t\t'tests/policy.test.ts',\n139\t\t'tests/config.test.ts',\n140\t\t'configs/helpers.ts',\n141\t\t'configs/policy.ts',\n142\t\t'.editorconfig',\n143\t\t'.gitattributes',\n144\t\t'.gitignore',\n145\t\t'.oxfmtrc.json',\n146\t\t'.oxlintrc.json',\n147\t\t'.oxlintignore',\n148\t\t'.prettierignore',\n149\t\t'guides/guide.md',\n150\t\t'guides/scaffold.md',\n151\t])\n152\t\n153\t/**\n154\t * The instruction-canon paths staged for reading rather than for a target, frozen.\n155\t *\n156\t * @remarks\n157\t * The root instruction documents, the orchestration contract every harness\n158\t * bridge points at, the rule map's rules, the skills, the templates, the\n159\t * transport contracts, the agent roles each harness dispatches, the bench\n160\t * configuration, and the MCP registrations. A directory entry covers everything\n161\t * beneath it.\n162\t *\n163\t * Staging walks these beside {@link HOST_PATHS}, so a release ships them and a\n164\t * reader reaches them two ways: a scaffold checkout sitting beside the\n165\t * repository, or the `node_modules/@orkestrel/scaffold/dist/host/` root inside\n166\t * the installed package. The `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` pointers scaffold plans\n167\t * are what name each location.\n168\t *\n169\t * These facts fix what a target holds at one of these paths.\n170\t *\n171\t * The lists are disjoint by prefix in either direction: no member of either\n172\t * equals or sits beneath a member of the other. Staging depends on that, because\n173\t * the walk covers the union and a path it discovers twice claims one storage\n174\t * name twice, which refuses the stage.\n175\t *\n176\t * The plan claims paths inside the canon deliberately, and each has a reason.\n177\t * `blueprintToDocumentArtifacts` claims `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` as this\n178\t * package's own template pointers. `nameToHostArtifacts` claims\n179\t * {@link CATALOG_AGENT_PATH}, because the catalog verb refuses a target that\n180\t * lacks the file and repair restores its absence.\n181\t *\n182\t * A target therefore holds a file at a canon path only where the plan claims it.\n183\t * That is the rule every verb obeys, and it is what makes a copy found anywhere\n184\t * else superseded.\n185\t */\n186\texport const CANON_PATHS: readonly string[] = Object.freeze([\n187\t\t'AGENTS.md',\n188\t\t'CLAUDE.md',\n189\t\t'.mcp.json',\n190\t\t'.agents/orchestration.md',\n191\t\t'.agents/skills',\n192\t\t'.agents/templates',\n193\t\t'.agents/transports',\n194\t\t'.claude/agents',\n195\t\t'.claude/rules',\n196\t\t'.claude/skills',\n197\t\t'.codex/agents',\n198\t\t'.codex/config.toml',\n199\t\t'.cursor/mcp.json',\n200\t\t'.cursor/rules',\n201\t])\n202\t\n203\t/** The repository-relative path where the committed vendored-file inventory is served. */\n204\texport const HOST_INVENTORY_PATH = 'host.json'\n205\t\n206\t/**\n207\t * The vendored paths whose present bytes belong to each workspace, frozen.\n208\t *\n209\t * @remarks"}]},"uuid":"cefe0e93-3f5c-4170-9e1d-f3c62b8eb505","timestamp":"2026-08-27T09:29:56.750Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"9807a69f-1af1-4986-91a6-ff67dbfb096e","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}

Repair may supply the hydrated\n635\t\t// plan it already needs for writes, while the public audit lets this method own\n636\t\t// the hydration itself.\n637\t\t#derive(plan: Plan, target: string, hydrated = this.#hydrate(plan)): Audit {\n638\t\t\tconst paths = new Set(hydrated.artifacts.map((artifact) => artifact.path))\n639\t\t\tfor (const root of this.#roots(plan)) {\n640\t\t\t\tconst directory = resolveContainedPath(target, root)\n641\t\t\t\tif (directory === undefined) {\n642\t\t\t\t\tthrow this.#error('TARGET', `The owned root at ${root} leaves its target.`, {\n643\t\t\t\t\t\ttarget,\n644\t\t\t\t\t\tpath: root,\n645\t\t\t\t\t})\n646\t\t\t\t}\n647\t\t\t\tfor (const name of listFiles(directory)) paths.add(`${root}/${name}`)\n648\t\t\t}\n649\t\t\tfor (const path of this.#canon(plan, target)) paths.add(path)\n650\t\t\treturn {\n651\t\t\t\tfindings: planToFindings(hydrated, readSnapshot(target, [...paths])),\n652\t\t\t\tquestions: [],\n653\t\t\t}\n654\t\t}\n655\t\n656\t\t// The canon paths a target actually holds. A release stages these for reading\n657\t\t// rather than for a target, so a copy sitting in one is an artifact of a release\n658\t\t// that vendored it, and reading it here is what lets the deletion verb take it.\n659\t\t//\n660\t\t// A directory member is read by file, which is what pairs the paths this plan\n661\t\t// claims inside the canon with their artifacts and leaves only the rest foreign,\n662\t\t// with no case for the planned file. The plan's own selection gates the reading,\n663\t\t// the same rule that decides a vendored path's group, so a scoped audit reports\n664\t\t// nothing outside its groups. A member that cannot resolve inside the target is\n665\t\t// not held by it.\n666\t\t#canon(plan: Plan, target: string): readonly string[] {\n667\t\t\tconst held: string[] = []\n668\t\t\tfor (const member of CANON_PATHS) {\n669\t\t\t\tconst full = resolveContainedPath(target, member)\n670\t\t\t\tif (full === undefined) continue\n671\t\t\t\tif (isPhysicalFile(full)) held.push(member)\n672\t\t\t\telse if (isPhysicalDirectory(full)) {\n673\t\t\t\t\tfor (const name of listFiles(full)) held.push(`${member}/${name}`)\n674\t\t\t\t}\n675\t\t\t}\n676\t\t\treturn held.filter((path) => plan.groups.includes(inferGroup(path)))\n677\t\t}\n678\t\n679\t\t// The target roots scaffold owns completely are exactly the host directories\n680\t\t// this plan expands. A manifest states the directory shape; a raw host is\n681\t\t// inspected directly. Files and host roots outside the plan never enter the set.\n682\t\t#roots(plan: Plan): readonly string[] {\n683\t\t\tconst roots = new Set<string>()\n684\t\t\tfor (const artifact of plan.artifacts) {\n685\t\t\t\tif (artifact.origin !== 'host') continue\n686\t\t\t\tconst source = artifact.source ?? artifact.path\n687\t\t\t\tif (this.#manifest?.roots.includes(source) === true) {\n688\t\t\t\t\troots.add(artifact.path)\n689\t\t\t\t\tcontinue\n690\t\t\t\t}\n691\t\t\t\tif (this.#manifest !== undefined) continue\n692\t\t\t\tconst root = this.#root\n693\t\t\t\tconst directory = root === undefined ? undefined : resolveContainedPath(root, source)\n694\t\t\t\tif (directory !== undefined && isPhysicalDirectory(directory)) roots.add(artifact.path)\n695\t\t\t}\n696\t\t\treturn [...roots]\n697\t\t}\n698\t\n699\t\t// One host artifact against the vendored root, through the manifest when the\n700\t\t// root carries one and one to one when it does not.\n701\t\t#expand(artifact: HostArtifact | HydratedArtifact, remaining: number): readonly Artifact[] {\n702\t\t\tconst manifest = this.#manifest\n703\t\t\tconst source = artifact.source ?? artifact.path\n704\t\t\tif (manifest === undefined) return this.#expandRaw(artifact, source, remaining)\n705\t\t\tconst matched = manifest.entries.filter(\n706\t\t\t\t(entry) => entry.destination === source || entry.destination.startsWith(`${source}/`),\n707\t\t\t)\n708\t\t\tconst rooted = manifest.roots.some((root) => root === source || root.startsWith(`${source}/`))\n709\t\t\tif (matched.length === 0 && !rooted) {\n710\t\t\t\tthrow this.#error('TARGET', `The vendored host does not carry ${source}.`, {\n711\t\t\t\t\thost: this.#root,\n712\t\t\t\t\tsource,\n713\t\t\t\t})\n714\t\t\t}\n715\t\t\tconst expanded: Artifact[] = []\n716\t\t\tlet budget = remaining\n717\t\t\tfor (const entry of matched) {\n718\t\t\t\tconst path = this.#remap(artifact, entry.destination)\n719\t\t\t\tif (WORKSPACE_OWNED_PATHS.includes(path)) {\n720\t\t\t\t\texpanded.push(this.#presence(artifact, path, entry.destination))\n721\t\t\t\t\tcontinue\n722\t\t\t\t}\n723\t\t\t\tif (isDeferredPath(path)) {\n724\t\t\t\t\texpanded.push(this.#presence(artifact, path, entry.destination))"}]},"uuid":"c0ce15c1-f748-4be5-9b50-bbef833541f5","timestamp":"2026-08-27T09:31:21.541Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"69d235bd-5108-4817-910f-71d917ea6d11","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}

{"parentUuid":"a7bb3dfd-6f22-440c-aafd-39db30c3ab97","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"8a9ea7b2-c6d7-44fa-ac15-131b6674fdae","agentId":"abee79fc04e41b5e9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01M5BYri1xJUcgigicUmXbiM","type":"tool_result","content":"1180\t * @param files - The host-owned vendored files read from the repository, one\n1181\t * row per path.\n1182\t * @param floor - The installed host floor, which fixes the membership a fill\n1183\t * may draw from and supplies the bytes owned by another surface.\n1184\t * @returns The assembled host, or `undefined` when any row produced no answer or\n1185\t * names a path the floor does not declare, or when a host-owned path is absent.\n1186\t *\n1187\t * @remarks\n1188\t * The one place the host-owned all-or-nothing rule is decided, so no verb\n1189\t * restates it. The host surface contributes one baseline: a fill carries live\n1190\t * bytes for every path that surface writes, or it is nothing. A row that failed,\n1191\t * went missing, names an undeclared path, or leaves a host-owned path absent\n1192\t * answers `undefined`. Deferred paths are presence-only and retain the installed\n1193\t * floor bytes that their catalog or mirror surface owns; repair never writes\n1194\t * those floor bytes. A canon path is read the same way for a different reason:\n1195\t * the canon is staged for reading rather than for a target, and the one canon\n1196\t * path a plan does claim is deferred, so the overlay never requests one and a\n1197\t * fill that carries no row for it is complete rather than spoiled. One `Host` can\n1198\t * therefore carry live host bytes beside floor bytes without mixing baselines\n1199\t * within a surface.\n1200\t *\n1201\t * The emitted entries keep the release's own order and its storage and\n1202\t * executable declarations, and carry digests recomputed over the bytes the fill\n1203\t * actually holds. That is what lets a reader verify the value against itself,\n1204\t * and it is why an undeclared path is refused rather than added: membership\n1205\t * moves with a release, never with a fetch.\n1206\t *\n1207\t * @example\n1208\t * ```ts\n1209\t * import { filesToHost } from '@orkestrel/scaffold/server'\n1210\t *\n1211\t * // A floor declaring the host-owned `scripts/codex.sh` path and the canon\n1212\t * // `AGENTS.md` destination. The script's live bytes are taken; the canon\n1213\t * // destination keeps the floor's.\n1214\t * filesToHost([{ path: 'scripts/codex.sh', lookup: 'found', hex: '23212f62696e2f73680a' }], floor)\n1215\t * // { manifest: { entries: [ … ], roots: [ … ], digest: '…' },\n1216\t * //   bytes: { 'scripts/codex.sh': '23212f62696e2f73680a', 'AGENTS.md': floor.bytes['AGENTS.md'] } }\n1217\t * ```\n1218\t */\n1219\texport function filesToHost(files: readonly HostFile[], floor: Host): Host | undefined {\n1220\t\tconst declared = new Set(floor.manifest.entries.map((entry) => entry.destination))\n1221\t\tconst held = new Map<string, string>()\n1222\t\tfor (const file of files) {\n1223\t\t\tif (file.lookup !== 'found' || !declared.has(file.path)) return undefined\n1224\t\t\tif (!isDeferredPath(file.path) && !isCanonPath(file.path)) held.set(file.path, file.hex)\n1225\t\t}\n1226\t\tconst entries: ManifestEntry[] = []\n1227\t\tconst bytes: Record<string, string> = {}\n1228\t\tfor (const entry of floor.manifest.entries) {\n1229\t\t\tconst hex ="}]},"uuid":"a7285c15-f963-4094-ba15-71cf27a8578d","timestamp":"2026-08-27T09:33:38.210Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"a7bb3dfd-6f22-440c-aafd-39db30c3ab97","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}

{"parentUuid":"cd3d9d5c-459b-4118-a2b2-097bf09aa0bf","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"8a9ea7b2-c6d7-44fa-ac15-131b6674fdae","agentId":"abee79fc04e41b5e9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01G3wLYCztX6NX3Cnd2CcAt4","type":"tool_result","content":"100\t  `.codex/config.toml`, `.mcp.json`, and `.cursor/mcp.json`; \"no plan claims those staged bytes, so\n101\t  no target receives a copy of a contract\" becomes \"a target receives a copy only where the plan\n102\t  claims the path\", with `CATALOG_AGENT_PATH` named beside the pointer pair.\n103\t- Disjointness: \"`HOST_PATHS` and `CANON_PATHS` are disjoint. A path in both would be copied into a\n104\t  target as a host artifact and refused by the overlay …\" → disjoint by prefix in either direction,\n105\t  with the storage-name reason; \"the executable's advisory\" → \"the executable's fetch list\"; and the\n106\t  closing sentence separates membership from being planned.\n107\t\n108\t**The move paragraph.**\n109\t\n110\t- Old: \"… where no artifact is planned at that path no verb writes or deletes that copy, so `audit`\n111\t  raises the `canon` question naming it until a maintainer removes it in one commit.\"\n112\t- New: \"… that copy sits at a path the plan does not own, so `audit` reports it `foreign` and exits\n113\t  `1`. `overwrite` deletes it in the run that repairs the pointers — one candidate list and one\n114\t  transaction, whether the file is a stray beneath a vendored directory or a superseded copy inside\n115\t  the canon. Membership decides that, never byte identity …\"\n116\t\n117\t**The fetch-list paragraph.**\n118\t\n119\t- Old: \"No target receives those bytes, so the fetch list drops the path rather than spending a round\n120\t  trip on one no verb can write.\"\n121\t- New: \"The fetch list drops it and `filesToHost` takes the installed floor bytes for it: a target\n122\t  receives no copy of a staged contract, and the one canon path a plan does claim is deferred, so\n123\t  live bytes reach neither.\"\n124\t\n125\t**The Limits entry (hunk `@@ -1499,15 +1506,17 @@`).**\n126\t\n127\t- Old heading and body: \"**No verb removes a superseded instruction copy.** … `audit` reports no\n128\t  drift against them and raises the non-blocking `canon` question naming them instead. The removal is\n129\t  one commit in the target — `git rm -r` over each named path …\"\n130\t- New heading and body: \"**A file a target keeps at a canon path never reports clean.** `overwrite`\n131\t  deletes a superseded instruction copy in the run that repairs the pointers, and it deletes only\n132\t  what git tracks, from a tree carrying no uncommitted work. An untracked copy is left standing, and\n133\t  a git-ignored one sits outside the dirty reading as well … The audit reads canon membership by\n134\t  path, so such a copy stays a `foreign` finding and that target exits `1` on every run. `repair`\n135\t  never closes it either … A maintainer who wants a local MCP server registration keeps it outside\n136\t  the repository, in the harness's own local or user scope, rather than at `.mcp.json`, where the\n137\t  file is drift whoever wrote it.\"\n138\t\n139\t### `README.md`\n140\t\n141\t- The split paragraph's vendored list becomes \"its toolchain, its policy proofs, its bench scripts,\n142\t  its harness permission file\"; the canon list gains \"the agent roles, the bench configuration, and\n143\t  the MCP registrations\"; the holdings sentence gains \"and the `.claude/agents/orkestrel.md` catalog\n144\t  file the `catalog` verb rewrites. Anything else a target holds at a canon path is a superseded\n145\t  copy, and `overwrite` deletes it.\"\n146\t- The `overwrite` passage gains: \"The deletion covers a stray beneath a vendored directory and a\n147\t  superseded instruction copy alike, so one run repairs the pointers and sweeps the canon paths a\n148\t  release moved.\"\n149\t\n150\t### `ROADMAP.md`\n151\t\n152\t- Old: \"re-pin `@orkestrel/scaffold` and install, run `repair` to take the `AGENTS.md` and\n153\t  `CLAUDE.md` pointers, delete each path the `canon` question names with `git rm -r`, and run the\n154\t  gates … the question self-extinguishes per target after the deletion commits.\"\n155\t- New: \"re-pin `@orkestrel/scaffold` and install, run `scaffold overwrite`, and run the gates. That\n156\t  one run takes the `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` pointers and deletes every tracked copy the target\n157\t  still holds at a canon path, so a second `scaffold audit` in the same visit exits `0`.\"\n158\t\n159\t### `.agents/skills/orkestrel-publish/references/wave.md`\n160\t\n161\tThe separate step 3 is deleted and the remaining steps renumber. Step 2 becomes:\n162\t\n163\t- \"Run `scaffold overwrite`. One run repairs the `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` pointers and deletes\n164\t  every tracked copy the target still holds at an instruction-canon path. Prove the sweep with a\n165\t  second `scaffold audit` that exits `0`.\"\n166\t- Sub-bullet: \"The deletion draws on what git tracks, so an untracked copy survives it, and the verb\n167\t  refuses the whole run as uncommitted work while an unignored one stands. Commit that copy or delete\n168\t  it by hand before re-running: `--dirty` clears the refusal and leaves the copy standing.\"\n169\t- Sub-bullet: \"A copy the target git-ignores stays a `foreign` finding, so that target never reaches\n170\t  exit `0` again.
