Opus wrote every commit under audit; the rewritten invariant sentences, the retired advisory's guide passage, the wave collapse, the re-baselined `prove` seam, and the coherence claim were attacked first and hardest.\n\n## Numbered verdicts\n\n**1. The membership is total and disjoint — CONFIRMED.**\nAttack tried: find a HOST member that sits beneath a CANON member, or a CANON member beneath a HOST member. `/home/user/scaffold/src/core/constants.ts:130-151` holds only files after the move (`LICENSE`, `.claude/settings.json`, four `scripts/*.sh`, three `tests/*.ts`, two `configs/*.ts`, seven root dotfiles, two `guides/*.md`); `.claude/settings.json` is a sibling of `.claude/agents`, `.claude/rules`, and `.claude/skills`, not beneath one. `CANON_PATHS` at `:186-201` carries the six wiring members beside the instruction set. `host.json` records `.codex/config.toml` (`:569`), `.cursor/mcp.json` (`:587`), and `.mcp.json` (`:617`), so the union stages. Held.\n\n**2. The plan's canon claims are exactly the pointer pair and the catalog file — CONFIRMED.**\nAttack tried: find a fourth claimed canon path, or a plan shape where `catalog` or `repair` loses the file. `tests/src/core/Compiler.test.ts` pins the set to `AGENTS.md:template`, `CLAUDE.md:template`, `.claude/agents/orkestrel.md:host`, and `nameToHostArtifacts` appends exactly one path (`src/core/compilers.ts:1554`). The `overwrite` end-to-end case asserts the consumer's prose above and below the markers survives and the table is rewritten; the repair case asserts the file is restored from staged bytes. Held.\n\n**3. An unplanned canon path is a group-scoped foreign finding — CONFIRMED.**\nAttack tried: reach `foreign` with a planned path, and make a scoped audit leak. `Materializer.#canon` (`src/server/Materializer.ts:666-677`) reads a directory member by file and filters through `plan.groups.includes(inferGroup(path))`, so a planned member pairs with its artifact before `planToFindings` runs. Both scoped cases carry the wide-selection control that fires on the same leftover. `.orkestrel/scaffold/probe-sweep.md` records `audit` exit 1 on three planted leftovers. Held.\n\n**4. One `overwrite` run sweeps clean — CONFIRMED.**\nAttack tried: find a spared tracked leftover or a taken planned path. One candidate list feeds one transaction; the `Materializer remove` case supplies `CATALOG_AGENT_PATH` and `AGENTS.md` in the tracked set and both survive while the two leftovers go, and the untracked `.mcp.json` lands in `skipped`. The probe records `3 removed`, `0 of 34 planned paths drifted`, and a clean offline re-audit. Deletion is by membership: no byte comparison appears on the path. Held. See the referral for a separate exit-code question the same probe raised.\n\n**5. `repair` deletes nothing — CONFIRMED.**\nAttack tried: find a repair path that reaches `remove`. The repair case asserts `removed` empty, the leftover byte-identical afterwards, and — as the control that separates \"removed nothing\" from \"wrote nothing\" — `written` containing `AGENTS.md`. The dirty refusal and `--dirty` waiver are untouched in the diff. Held.\n\n**6. The advisory is fully retired — CONFIRMED.**\nAttack tried: find a surviving `canon` question value, a dead union member, or a fetch-list regression. `#canonQuestion` and its call site are gone; `field` on `Question` is a free string, so no literal is stranded. A grep of `src/` for `'canon'` returns comment prose only. The fetch filter still reads `!isDeferredPath(...) && !isCanonPath(...)` (`src/bin/CLI.ts:635-641`) and `filesToHost` still skips both (`src/server/helpers.ts:1224`). Held.\n\n**7. `host.json` moved exactly with the staged files the follow-up edited — CONFIRMED.**\nAttack tried: find an added, removed, or reordered entry hiding among the digest churn. The whole `host.json` hunk set is four digest replacements — `agents/skills/orkestrel-publish/references/wave.md`, `claude/agents/orkestrel.md`, `claude/rules/quality.md`, `guides/scaffold.md` — plus the membership digest. `README.md` and `ROADMAP.md` are in neither list, so their edits correctly move nothing. The `roots` array is unchanged. Held.\n\n**8. No file scaffold plans into a target names a path the target lacks — CONFIRMED, bounded to a generated target.**\nAttack tried: read every planned body against a fresh target. The `AGENTS.md` pointer (`src/core/templates.ts:2022-2049`) names `.claude/agents/orkestrel.md`, `.claude/settings.json`, and `scripts/`, all of which a default plan claims. `.claude/settings.json` hooks name `scripts/deps.sh`, `scripts/ollama.sh`, `scripts/cursor.sh`, `scripts/codex.sh`, and every one is in `HOST_PATHS`; no script names a `.cursor`, `.codex`, `.mcp.json`, or `.claude` path. Held for a target. The same file is false in scaffold's own checkout — finding B.\n\n**9. The shipped prose is true — BROKEN.**\n\nFalsifying input: `guides/scaffold.md:1226-1227`.\n\n> \"…a target receives no copy of a staged contract, and the one canon path a plan does claim is deferred, so live bytes reach neither.\"\n\nThe plan claims **three** canon paths, and two of them are not deferred. `tests/src/core/Compiler.test.ts` pins the claimed canon set to `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, and `.claude/agents/orkestrel.md`; `isDeferredPath` (`src/core/helpers.ts:189-190`) is true only for the catalog file and `guides/*.md`, and its own TSDoc example at `:186` states `isDeferredPath('AGENTS.md') // false`. The same commit's `src/core/helpers.ts:206-209` says it correctly — \"A plan claims `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, and {@link CATALOG_AGENT_PATH} at canon paths deliberately\" — so the package now contradicts itself between two files edited in one change.\n\nSame false sentence, same commit, at `src/server/helpers.ts:1195-1196`, where the TSDoc `@example` twelve lines later uses `AGENTS.md` as \"the canon destination\" whose floor bytes are kept — the example demonstrates the second claimed canon path the prose says does not exist. The fixture prose repeats the shape at `tests/setupServer.ts:1113` and `:1134`.\n\nWhy it matters: this is the invariant sentence the follow-up rewrote, and it is the reason a reader is given for why a fill missing a canon row is complete. The mechanism is right — `isCanonPath` covers all three — so a reader who trusts the stated reason will conclude that adding a second claimed canon path is safe only if it is deferred, which is not the rule the code obeys.\n\nWhat right looks like: state the rule the filter actually implements. \"The fetch list drops every canon destination and `filesToHost` takes the installed floor bytes for it, whether the plan claims the path or not, so live bytes reach none of them.\" Apply the same correction at `src/server/helpers.ts:1195`, and drop the \"one destination\" phrasing at `tests/setupServer.ts:1113` and `:1134` in favour of naming the vendored claim.\n\nThe rest of claim 9 held: no sentence anywhere claims a verb never removes a superseded copy (the Limits heading is now \"A file a target keeps at a canon path never reports clean\"), the `prove` seam is stated as registration outside the repository with the in-tree consequence documented, and the visit reads re-pin → `scaffold overwrite` → gates in `ROADMAP.md`, `wave.md`, and the guide alike.\n\n**10. The law holds across the diff — BROKEN.**\n\nFalsifying input: `AGENTS.md` § Writing — \"**NEVER state a count.** A number answering 'how many' about a set anyone can add to is a count.\"\n\n\"the one canon path a plan does claim\" is a count over the set of canon paths the plan claims, at `guides/scaffold.md:1226` and `src/server/helpers.ts:1195`. \"It is the one destination a plan claims inside the canon\" at `tests/setupServer.ts:1134` and \"A plan claims one destination inside the canon\" at `:1113` are the same count. The law's remedy applies directly: name the members, or write the sentence without the number — which is also the repair claim 9 needs.\n\nSecond violation, `src/core/constants.ts:169`:\n\n> \"These facts fix what a target holds at one of these paths.\"\n\n`.claude/rules/writing.md` § Sentence and paragraph order requires naming the noun after `these` where a reader could attach it elsewhere; here `These facts` has no antecedent in the preceding paragraph, which is about where a reader reaches the canon, not about what a target holds. The line states no rule, no trigger, and no check, and it sits alone between two paragraphs — it reads as residue from an edit that moved its content. Delete it, or replace it with the sentence it was meant to introduce.\n\nThe rest of claim 10 held: no `any`, no `as`, no non-null assertion, no suppression directive anywhere in the diff; `#canon` is correctly a private method reaching `#` state rather than an extracted helper; `commitFiles` follows `{verb}{Noun}`; the deleted `matchesVacantRoot` and `FLEET_WRITE_COUNT` leave no stranded consumer; every public export the diff touches keeps its TSDoc and its barrel row.\n\n**11. The package is coherent as a whole — BROKEN.**\n\nFalsifying state: a target generated or swept by this release.\n\nThe concept the design names is \"instruction canon … published for reading\", and the pointer pair is the mechanism that makes reading work. That mechanism does not extend to the six paths this follow-up moved. `.claude/agents/`, `.codex/agents/`, `.codex/config.toml`, `.cursor/mcp.json`, `.cursor/rules/`, and `.mcp.json` are not read by a person following a pointer — they are loaded by a harness from a fixed location in the repository root. Moving them into the canon does not relocate where they are read from; it removes them.\n\nThe contradiction is entirely in-tree and needs no claim about any external tool. `.agents/orchestration.md` is canon, it is the file the `AGENTS.md` pointer template names as required reading, and it states:\n\n- \"Give every role a file on both sides. The role file is where engine, effort, tools, permissions, and charter are pinned, and the tool allowlist is what makes the read-only floor real. A role with no file has nowhere to pin either.\"\n- \"Reach every role by its own name. Do not rely on a remembered route.\"\n- and a roles table whose Claude column is `.claude/agents/` and whose Codex column is `.codex/agents/`.\n\nAfter this release a target holds exactly one file in `.claude/agents/` — `orkestrel.md`, kept for a tooling reason (`catalog` refuses a target that lacks it), not for the reason the contract requires — and nothing in `.codex/agents/`. `.orkestrel/scaffold/probe-sweep.md` records the generated target: \"`.codex`, `.cursor`, `.mcp.json` absent; `.claude/agents` holds orkestrel.md\". So the contract every target is pointed at requires role files on both sides, and the tree every target receives has one of them.\n\nThis is not a pre-existing condition the follow-up inherited. Before `1a2f04e` these paths were in `HOST_PATHS` and every target carried them. After publication `overwrite` will delete them from every target that still has them, in the same run the wave reference tells a maintainer to run.\n\nThe incoherence compounds inside the vendored set the release keeps. `README.md:11` now advertises what a target carries as \"its toolchain, its policy proofs, **its bench scripts**, its harness permission file\", and `.claude/settings.json` still runs `scripts/cursor.sh` and `scripts/codex.sh` at every session start and still allows `mcp__codex__codex` and `mcp__probe__prove`. A target is therefore left holding the launchers and the permissions for two benches while holding none of the wiring those benches need and none of the roles that would be dispatched across them.\n\nNo shipped file states this cost. I read `guides/scaffold.md` (the Limits entry, the Git section, the groups table, the vendored-data-root section), `README.md`, `ROADMAP.md`, `wave.md`, and the `AGENTS.md` pointer template. The only seam any of them names is the MCP one, and it names it as a prohibition — `guides/scaffold.md:1516` tells a maintainer to keep a registration \"outside the repository, in the harness's own local or user scope, rather than at `.mcp.json`, where the file is drift whoever wrote it.\" By the same rule a project-scoped role file is drift whoever wrote it, so the release closes the door on per-repository role definition without saying so anywhere.\n\nWhat right looks like, without reopening the owner's scope ruling that the wiring joins the canon:\n\n1. `guides/scaffold.md` Limits gains an entry stating what a target loses at the split — that after this release a target holds no dispatchable agent role beyond the catalog file, no bench configuration, and no MCP registration, and that a harness in a target reaches none of the canon copies inside `node_modules`.\n2. That entry names where those definitions live for a target instead — the harness's own user scope, matching the ruling already made for `.mcp.json` — or records that the fleet's targets are not orchestration hosts.\n3.
